This would allow future students to undertake basic military training during the holidays after finishing high school and then join the contractual reserve or work in the Slovenian Army as professional soldiers. Novela also solves the problem of ageing staff in the Slovenian Army. A supplement is proposed, with which even after 45 years of age, soldiers with specialized knowledge could continue their work in the Slovenian Army. They also want to retain other members who could be redeployed to protect sites of special defence importance; and this would relieve younger soldiers. This category of soldiers could also be redeployed to the post of police officer of the state border or judicial officer. They will also have the possibility of deploying to the relevant posts at the Ministry of Defence or other state bodies, while retaining the number of wage classes achieved. To conclude, in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we are convinced that the profession of the soldier could be made more attractive also with additional material benefits for members of the Slovenian Armed Forces, such as the favourable rental of apartments, the supplement for availability, special cash awards at the annual level, the protection of children, financial compensation for the performance of tasks outside the working time, the purchase of certain goods without tax, the reduction of the required level of education for employment in the Slovenian Army and similar other solutions. For all the reasons mentioned above, we will support the Draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Defence Act in the Parliamentary Group of the Modern Centre.

Even if this amendment of yours is accepted, no one will still check whether they are members of political parties, because we have a regulation on the contractual performance of military service in the reserve composition of the Slovenian Armed Forces, where the exact conditions for who may be a reserve are laid down. And the reservist may be someone who is a citizen of the Republic of Slovenia and who does not have dual citizenship, who is physically and mentally capable of carrying out military service, who has an adequate education for soldiers, which is normally a secondary vocational school, that they have been, in accordance with the regulations — very important — safely verified and there is no security reservation, that they are not legally convicted of an offence pursued under official duty, and have not been sentenced to unconditional imprisonment for any other offence for more than three months, that they are not employed abroad or that the employer has not directed them to perform work, training or to education abroad for more than three months. Moreover, those who have been recognised as conscientious objectors and those who have been considered unfit for military service as recruits of military service may not be reservists. The conditions for a reservist are extremely strict, so any fears that you take with you are completely out of line. Under these conditions, there is no record of any need to make a declaration that he is not a member of political parties. In a way, your amendment in this sense is irrelevant.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-09-29-SDZ8-Redna-20.ana.u96	Nice greeting also Slovenian Army, all soldiers and, of course, all those who care about the safety of Slovenia. The National Assembly has one floor below a very nice library. There are some constitutional law textbooks in there. I like to read them myself, especially the one written by Prof. Dr. Lovro Šturm about what is the limitation of power, what is the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms and how the Slovenian Constitution regulates it. How nice it would be and how we could avoid unnecessary debate today, and even in the days before, if at least some basics were known by everyone. What needs to be known about the Slovenian Constitution is that it has a special chapter governing human rights and fundamental freedoms; and that it has, then, in Article 15 of this Chapter, a special guardian who provides, inter alia, that human rights and fundamental freedoms are exercised directly on the basis of the Constitution itself, that they are not allowed to be further restricted and that they are not allowed to be restricted in any way, nor by an excuse that the Constitution does not recognise them or that it recognises them to a lesser extent. Why is that important? Because that's the difference between democracy and totalitarianism. Totalitarianism is a negative concept of law, only what is explicitly allowed, which is written down; everything else is prohibited. We live in democracy, in the constitutional Republic, where we have a positive right to allow anything that is not exceptionally prohibited. And only the Constitution limits certain rights and fundamental freedoms, and in precisely certain cases. And in specific cases, it also allows this implementation or implementation to be regulated by law. In the specific case in question, the Constitution is crystal clear in the last paragraph of Article 42; the restriction on membership in political parties applies only to professional members of the armed forces and the police, says the military and the police. It's perfectly clear who he's referring to. No expansion is allowed here. The Constitution as a fundamental human right gives the right to unification. It also provides as a fundamental right of freedom in this Chapter that everyone has the right to participate in public affairs. And so on. All of this would be questioned if what is limited in the Constitution were to be extended to another. And here it is clear – professional only applies this restriction. The reserve composition is not professional, it is contractual, they are not professionally bound as career Slovenian soldiers or career Slovenian police officers to this institution, they cannot in any case be subject to this restriction. Moreover, if someone wanted to implement this, it would then fly with certainty either in the Constitutional Court or in the European Court of Human Rights, because it would constitute an unnecessary and, under the very Article of the Constitution, which I quoted earlier, article 15, an additional inadmissible restriction on human rights and fundamental freedoms. It's so simple, really. That is why this whole debate is unnecessary. The restriction in the Constitution applies to those who are professionally, careerally, with their lives committed to careers in the police or the military. It does not apply to others and cannot apply. And whoever draws from it I do not know what conclusion and says otherwise, but it shows more ignorance. This does unnecessary damage to the military itself, to the police and, on the other hand, to double criteria, since such a article is already enshrined in a law governing the police area, and there is no problem, it is said to be completely indisputable. And the same article is now provided for if it is, or if it is not, in any case, the Constitution applies. Once again, only those restrictions on human rights and fundamental freedoms in the Constitution are permissible; and only the regulation of those areas where the Constitution provides for regulation by law is permissible. No other thing is permissible, we live in positive law, which means that it is not explicitly prohibited in the field of human rights and fundamental freedoms under the Constitution, is allowed, is the right of every individual; and no one has the right to do so in any way. I think that as far as it's concerned, it's perfectly clear, it's over. In short, the law is well prepared and I support it. On the other hand, I'd like to point out something else. The law – such as – represents a certain investment, an investment in people, in Slovenian soldiers. Even in this part, when it regulates when it allows them an appropriate solution after the age of 45, to solve one problem they have faced in an appropriate way. And this system is now comparable to many other countries, where this is applicable and has been going well for many years. Today, when we look at the modern world, we must be aware of that fundamental message, which always applies when we talk about the security system in which the Slovenian Army is, of course, a pillar. Security investment is today an investment in development. Only those countries that are capable of providing themselves and their citizens with permanent, indivisible, comprehensive security will develop, those are perspectives, capital will come in, investments will come in, all others will start to be poor. One who doubts whether he should look at the richest country or at least one of the richest countries is Sweden; what does totalitarianism do or, if you want, socialism, look at Venezuela. The message is clear.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-09-29-SDZ8-Redna-20.ana.u153	Thank you very much. The draft law was considered by the Defence Committee as the working body of the mother. I give the floor to President Sam Bevk to present the report to the Committee. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2020-09-29-SDZ8-Redna-20.ana.u154	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. A respected choir! At its 18th emergency meeting on 3 September 2020, the Defence Committee considered the proposal for a law on the provision of investment funds in the Slovenian Army from 2021 to 2026, the second hearing sent by the Government to the National Assembly. At its 43rd extraordinary meeting on 16 July 2020, the National Assembly adopted a conclusion that the draft law was appropriate for further consideration. On behalf of the applicant, the Minister for the Defence of Magi presented the introductory addition to the explanation of the draft law. Matthew Tonin, who presented the main objectives and main solutions to the bill. The basic objective of the proposal for a law on the provision of investment funds in the Slovenian Army between 2021 and 2026 is to provide funding on a planned scale and following the planned dynamics for the establishment of the most important military capacity to fulfil the tasks of the Slovenian Armed Forces and to participate in the collective defence system. The law will provide funds to make the most important investments in the Slovenian Army in the years 2021-2026. The law will regulate spending of €780 million, with annual spending of €100 million over the first two years and 145 million per year over the next four years. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service, Helena Rus, said that the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia had examined the draft law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, the legal system and the legislative and technical aspects and had made a comment on Article 4 of the Law, which stipulated that the law was not limited in time. Slovenian Army Chief of Staff Robert Glavas pointed out that there has been virtually no investment in the personnel, equipment and infrastructure of the Slovenian Army in the past 12 years. He stressed, however, that as Chief of Staff for such investments, it is only possible to carry out the tasks of the Slovenian Armed Forces as defined in the strategic documents and to turn the development curve of the Slovenian Armed Forces from regressive to progressive. However, the President of the Commission of the National Council of State, Rajko Fajt, said that the Commission was in favour of the bill. In the debate, the proposaler and the discussors of the coalition of parliamentary groups highlighted their preference for the modernisation and strengthening of the Slovenian Armed Forces, while the opposition's representatives were detained in their performances or opposed to the bill. The opposition MPs took the lead in evading the coalition's representatives by proposing a bill and avoiding a broader public debate or public presentation of opinions. On the contrary, members of the coalition rejected the allegations that the Government intended to invest heavily in the military at the expense of investing in health care and other subsystems, pointing out that it was not a divisive health or safety system because the government would take care of both. In accordance with the second paragraph of Article 130 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee considered and adopted amendments to Article 4 of the SDS, SMC, NSI and Desus. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia, the Committee voted on the articles of the bill and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation, which includes the amendments adopted, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended draft law is an integral part of this report. Thank you very much.

By law on the provision of investment funds in the Slovenian Army in the years 2021 to 2026, the Slovenian armies are restoring the dignity and the city they belong to and deserve in Slovenian society. The Slovenian army consists of Slovenian girls and boys who have dressed up military uniforms for unconditional service of our only country. In Novi Slovenija we are aware that the Slovenian soldier, with the constant reduction of the defence budget, cannot be well trained or equipped, thereby endangering his life and reducing the seriousness of the defence of the Republic of Slovenia. We have been late for 15 years in Slovenia with such investments, so there is absolutely no justification to make such purchases fast. The words of the former Chief-General of the Slovenian Army, Major General Alen Geder, remind us that if we do not take a timely systemic action, we will very soon not have the Slovenian Army. Defence Minister Matthew Tonin is aware of the weight of the burden he was entrusted with, so he started a wide dialogue and debate at the temple of democracy on 24 June this year. He visited all the parliamentary groups that wanted to accept him, only the Left Group did not find the courage to meet and talk. But the door stays open. The necessary law will provide funds for the implementation of the most important investments in the Slovenian Army, in the first two years after 100 million euros and in the next four years after 145 million euros. With this law, the Government follows only the fundamental defence and strategic documents adopted by the previous left governments, including the supra-government party Levica at the helm. However, the Minister of Defence did not want to remain only in his words and decided to carry them out. It did not order new and expensive and overpaid studies or strategies, but took from the drawer the white book of the previous government, where it was determined that the complete equipment of the Slovenian Army would require more than EUR 3 billion. The Ministry of Defence does not have this money, but they have managed to narrow down and optimize the necessary equipment. The law is transparent and clearly indicates a vision of means of investment in the Slovenian Army. In Novi Slovenije – Christian Democrats, we claim that the proposed investments in the Slovenian Army are in the current composition the minimum we need to ensure if we want to maintain the Slovenian Army. Security is not free, but in New Slovenia we are convinced that NATO is the cheapest insurance policy. The defence expenditure of non-NATO countries, such as Austria and Switzerland, is significantly higher. Military neutrality does not mean less, but more spending on defense. By 2030, neutral Switzerland will only spend 7.4 billion euros on new aircraft and long-range ground defence systems. So the Slovenians at the referendum on the accession to the relationship then decided wisely, but also rationally. Slovenia wants to be a reliable member of the international community. It is in Slovenia's strategic interest to ensure international peace and stability. Our most numerous mission, three quarters of all troops on missions, is not at other ends of the world, but in the Balkans, because we are aware of the seriousness of the situation there. Slovenia wants to be a credible partner in NATO and we have therefore committed ourselves in previous governments to increasing the defence budget and equipment of the Slovenian Army. The well-equipped and human resources-completed Slovenian Army is not the last thing we need because of ourselves. Although we do not feel securely threatened within the country, the situation is slightly different at our Schengen border, from where I come from, and a few more hours of driving away, we enter into a much different, security very uncertain situation. In New Slovenia – we do not deceive Christian Democrats citizens and citizens with lies and populism. We will never agree to the demagogy that some are spreading — that it is necessary to decide between health and cannons. There is no dilemma for New Slovenia. We'll take care of both in the current government. Much of this will also be achieved through the EUR 10.5 billion European funds. Slovenia and the people at the border need a well-equipped army. After many years of inactivity of the former Defence Ministers, Matthew Tonin, with the law on the provision of investment funds in the Slovenian Army, finally restores dignity and the future of a key security force in our country. That's why I'm going to support this marriage. Thank you for your word.

Manufacturers of such technological advanced equipment shall not produce such advanced equipment in stock, but for a known buyer, after the contract has been signed, with specified specifications, versions, characteristics of the equipment agreed in the contract. The anticipated delivery deadline for more demanding military systems is also 18 months or more from the date of signature of the contract. In addition, provision is made for the higher value of the project and the dynamics of production generally divided over a longer period of time, from two to three years from the first delivery, since the total investment simply cannot be made financially in one financial year or budget period. In the case of military equipment contracts, this is specific equipment, which is also subject to specific procurement rules as defined by Directive 2009/81/EU or the national law on public procurement in the field of defence and security adopted by the National Assembly. But the law in Levica, after consulting the parliamentary groups, was designed so that, on one hand, 30,000 workers and workers working on Sundays could stay at home and on the other hand small shops could operate if, on Sundays, there were no employees working there, but managers, prosecutors and other legal representatives of companies. In order to achieve this, we will support the first amendment we have proposed in Levica, which allows what I have just described, saying that small stores are open even on Sundays if managers and not workers work there. We have passed this on from good practice abroad, especially in the Swiss example. However, we will vote against the amendments of the SMC, which are tabled because these two amendments introduce additional exceptions to the law, extending work to family members and restricting work to 10 weeks a year, which are not well explained and there is a risk that they will be subject to a dispute in the Constitutional Court or that they will be the reason for the law to fall in the wake of a possible constitutional assessment. So, as we say, the first amendment, our amendment will be supported, and the two amendments to the SMC will be rejected.

We are moving on to the examination of the motion for election to the judicial office of the District Judge at the District Court in Novi mesto. The Council of Justice submitted to the National Assembly a proposal for the election of Petr Žnidaršič as Judge of the District Court of Justice in the New Town District Court. The proposal was addressed as the parent working body by the Mandate-Electorial Commission, which informed the assembly in writing. There are no applications for discussion. We are moving on to a decision on a motion for a decision which elects Peter Žnidaršič as judge of the District Court of Justice in the New Town District Court. We're voting. There are 85 Members and Members present, 80 votes against none. [[The vote was 80.]] [[No one.]] I understand that the decision has been taken. We are moving on to the consideration of the Proposal for a Decision on the composition and appointment of the President, Vice-President, Members and Deputy Members of the Commission of Inquiry on the provision of protective equipment and measures by institutions and holders of public functions to combat the spread of COPID-19 disease from 1. 2. 2020 until the purpose of the parliamentary inquiry in question. The proposal for a decision was submitted by the Mandate-Electorial Commission for consideration by the assembly. There are no applications for discussion. We are moving on to a decision on the proposal for a decision on the composition and appointment of the President, the Vice-President, members and alternate members of that Commission of Inquiry. We're voting. There are 85 Members and Members present, with 73 votes against 7. [[The vote was 73.]] [[Against the seven.]] I understand that the decision has been taken. I hereby conclude this agenda item and the 20th meeting of the National Assembly.
